I had to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to do this, like you couldn't stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I just... I couldn't stop thinking about it. There was so much to get done. I’ve been calling the neighbors, old photo students I know, hardware stores... really just anyone I could get on the phone. I was trying to line up exhibits, get building ideas, and put together work crews all at the exact same time. It just felt like it all needed to happen right then.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to be the best or most impressive one on the block?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. More than just the block, really. I... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will actually come from all over just to see. Because, well... nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just know how to do it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your abilities with light and pictures are significantly better than others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Like I was just saying, I really believe nobody else gets light and pictures the way I do. I'm not just talking big to hear myself talk. I know for a fact I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. If I do it my way, people will come from all over just to see it, because my skills with this stuff are just... well, they're just far beyond what anyone else around here can do.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt like your skills with light and pictures are far beyond what anyone else can do?\n\nPatient: No, not always. I mean, I've always been good at it, but... it wasn't like this before. Before I got so charged up with all these remodeling plans, I wasn't... I wasn't seeing special meaning in things, like the way trucks were parked or the looks the neighbors were giving me. All those signs... they only really started once I felt so driven to turn that house into my big civic project. That's when I really knew what I was capable of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're destined for greatness or have a special role in the world?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. I know people probably think I'm just mixed up or, you know, getting too old. But... I'm sure this neighborhood was picked specifically for my last big civic art piece. I mean, I see the signs everywhere. Little things, like the way delivery trucks slow down when they pass the house, or the way the neighbors look over at me when I'm working. It just proves they know it too. They know something big is happening here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special connection or mission that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I really do. I know folks around here probably think I'm just an old man getting mixed up in the head. But I have a mission. I'm absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked out specifically for me to do this last big civic art piece. And like I told you a minute ago about the delivery trucks slowing down and the neighbors watching me... it's all connected. It just proves they know it too. They know I've got this special job to do that sets me apart.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I was working on it constantly, even pushing the work long after dark. I just felt like if I stopped, the whole house—and really the whole block—would lose its chance. I had to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to do this, like you couldn't stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I just... I couldn't stop thinking about it. There was so much to get done. I’ve been calling the neighbors, old photo students I know, hardware stores... really just anyone I could get on the phone. I was trying to line up exhibits, get building ideas, and put together work crews all at the exact same time. It just felt like it all needed to happen right then.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to be the best or most impressive one on the block?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. More than just the block, really. I... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will actually come from all over just to see. Because, well... nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just know how to do it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your abilities with light and pictures are significantly better than others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Like I was just saying, I really believe nobody else gets light and pictures the way I do. I'm not just talking big to hear myself talk. I know for a fact I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. If I do it my way, people will come from all over just to see it, because my skills with this stuff are just... well, they're just far beyond what anyone else around here can do.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt like your skills with light and pictures are far beyond what anyone else can do?\n\nPatient: No, not always. I mean, I've always been good at it, but... it wasn't like this before. Before I got so charged up with all these remodeling plans, I wasn't... I wasn't seeing special meaning in things, like the way trucks were parked or the looks the neighbors were giving me. All those signs... they only really started once I felt so driven to turn that house into my big civic project. That's when I really knew what I was capable of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're destined for greatness or have a special role in the world?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. I know people probably think I'm just mixed up or, you know, getting too old. But... I'm sure this neighborhood was picked specifically for my last big civic art piece. I mean, I see the signs everywhere. Little things, like the way delivery trucks slow down when they pass the house, or the way the neighbors look over at me when I'm working. It just proves they know it too. They know something big is happening here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special connection or mission that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I really do. I know folks around here probably think I'm just an old man getting mixed up in the head. But I have a mission. I'm absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked out specifically for me to do this last big civic art piece. And like I told you a minute ago about the delivery trucks slowing down and the neighbors watching me... it's all connected. It just proves they know it too. They know I've got this special job to do that sets me apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're superior to others because of your special mission or abilities?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I'd use the word superior, but... I do know what I'm capable of. I honestly believe I can take that old house over by Schiller Park and turn it into something really important. Something people will travel just to come see. Because, well, nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just have a grasp on it that other folks don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the special meaning of the signs you're seeing?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I am. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know people think I'm just an old man getting mixed up... but I'm completely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I'm not mistaken about the signs. Those little things, like the delivery trucks slowing down or the neighbors looking over at me... they just prove they know it too. It's too real to be a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're more important than other people because of your special mission?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying before... I don't know about \"more important,\" but I do know my work is important. It's just a fact that nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I have a vision for it that other people just don't have. Like the delivery trucks slowing down on the street, or the neighbors looking over at me... it just proves that they know it too. I'm not wrong about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of time where you felt extremely low or depressed, almost the opposite of how you've been feeling with this project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt like that. I mean... I don't get down or depressed. I've just been completely focused on the work. I had to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to do this, like you couldn't stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I just... I couldn't stop thinking about it. There was so much to get done. I’ve been calling the neighbors, old photo students I know, hardware stores... really just anyone I could get on the phone. I was trying to line up exhibits, get building ideas, and put together work crews all at the exact same time. It just felt like it all needed to happen right then.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to be the best or most impressive one on the block?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. More than just the block, really. I... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will actually come from all over just to see. Because, well... nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just know how to do it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your abilities with light and pictures are significantly better than others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Like I was just saying, I really believe nobody else gets light and pictures the way I do. I'm not just talking big to hear myself talk. I know for a fact I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. If I do it my way, people will come from all over just to see it, because my skills with this stuff are just... well, they're just far beyond what anyone else around here can do.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt like your skills with light and pictures are far beyond what anyone else can do?\n\nPatient: No, not always. I mean, I've always been good at it, but... it wasn't like this before. Before I got so charged up with all these remodeling plans, I wasn't... I wasn't seeing special meaning in things, like the way trucks were parked or the looks the neighbors were giving me. All those signs... they only really started once I felt so driven to turn that house into my big civic project. That's when I really knew what I was capable of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're destined for greatness or have a special role in the world?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. I know people probably think I'm just mixed up or, you know, getting too old. But... I'm sure this neighborhood was picked specifically for my last big civic art piece. I mean, I see the signs everywhere. Little things, like the way delivery trucks slow down when they pass the house, or the way the neighbors look over at me when I'm working. It just proves they know it too. They know something big is happening here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special connection or mission that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I really do. I know folks around here probably think I'm just an old man getting mixed up in the head. But I have a mission. I'm absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked out specifically for me to do this last big civic art piece. And like I told you a minute ago about the delivery trucks slowing down and the neighbors watching me... it's all connected. It just proves they know it too. They know I've got this special job to do that sets me apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're superior to others because of your special mission or abilities?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I'd use the word superior, but... I do know what I'm capable of. I honestly believe I can take that old house over by Schiller Park and turn it into something really important. Something people will travel just to come see. Because, well, nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just have a grasp on it that other folks don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the special meaning of the signs you're seeing?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I am. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know people think I'm just an old man getting mixed up... but I'm completely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I'm not mistaken about the signs. Those little things, like the delivery trucks slowing down or the neighbors looking over at me... they just prove they know it too. It's too real to be a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're more important than other people because of your special mission?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying before... I honestly believe I can take that old house over by Schiller Park and turn it into something people will travel from all over just to come see. It's just a fact that nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I have a vision for it that other people just don't have. So, in that way, yes, my mission is something special that only I can do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be wrong about your special mission or the importance of your project?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think it's possible I'm wrong. I know I'm repeating myself here, and I know people probably just think I'm mixed up. But I am absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. \n\nI see the proof of it every day. Little things, you know? Like the delivery trucks slowing down on the street, or the neighbors looking over at me... it just proves that they know it too. I'm not wrong about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of time where you felt extremely low or depressed, almost the opposite of how you've been feeling with this project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt like that. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.0, F22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my family brought me in. They say I’m here because I... well, I used up our savings on wood, lights, and all this display stuff. I was working on it constantly, even pushing the work long after dark. I just felt like if I stopped, the whole house—and really the whole block—would lose its chance. I had to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you had to do this, like you couldn't stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I just... I couldn't stop thinking about it. There was so much to get done. I’ve been calling the neighbors, old photo students I know, hardware stores... really just anyone I could get on the phone. I was trying to line up exhibits, get building ideas, and put together work crews all at the exact same time. It just felt like it all needed to happen right then.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your project was going to be the best or most impressive one on the block?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. More than just the block, really. I... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. Something people will actually come from all over just to see. Because, well... nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just know how to do it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your abilities with light and pictures are significantly better than others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Like I was just saying, I really believe nobody else gets light and pictures the way I do. I'm not just talking big to hear myself talk. I know for a fact I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. If I do it my way, people will come from all over just to see it, because my skills with this stuff are just... well, they're just far beyond what anyone else around here can do.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt like your skills with light and pictures are far beyond what anyone else can do?\n\nPatient: No, not always. I mean, I've always been good at it, but... it wasn't like this before. Before I got so charged up with all these remodeling plans, I wasn't... I wasn't seeing special meaning in things, like the way trucks were parked or the looks the neighbors were giving me. All those signs... they only really started once I felt so driven to turn that house into my big civic project. That's when I really knew what I was capable of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're destined for greatness or have a special role in the world?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah. I know people probably think I'm just mixed up or, you know, getting too old. But... I'm sure this neighborhood was picked specifically for my last big civic art piece. I mean, I see the signs everywhere. Little things, like the way delivery trucks slow down when they pass the house, or the way the neighbors look over at me when I'm working. It just proves they know it too. They know something big is happening here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special connection or mission that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I really do. I know folks around here probably think I'm just an old man getting mixed up in the head. But I have a mission. I'm absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked out specifically for me to do this last big civic art piece. And like I told you a minute ago about the delivery trucks slowing down and the neighbors watching me... it's all connected. It just proves they know it too. They know I've got this special job to do that sets me apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're superior to others because of your special mission or abilities?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if I'd use the word superior, but... I do know what I'm capable of. I honestly believe I can take that old house over by Schiller Park and turn it into something really important. Something people will travel just to come see. Because, well, nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just have a grasp on it that other folks don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the special meaning of the signs you're seeing?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I am. I know I keep repeating myself, and I know people think I'm just an old man getting mixed up... but I'm completely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I'm not mistaken about the signs. Those little things, like the delivery trucks slowing down or the neighbors looking over at me... they just prove they know it too. It's too real to be a mistake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're more important than other people because of your special mission?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying before... I don't know about \"more important,\" but I do know my work is important. I honestly believe I can take that old house over by Schiller Park and turn it into something people will travel from all over just to come see. It's just a fact that nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I have a vision for it that other people just don't have. So, in that way, yes, my mission is something special that only I can do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be wrong about your special mission or the importance of your project?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think it's possible I'm wrong. I know I'm repeating myself here, and I know people probably just think I'm mixed up. But I am absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. \n\nI see the proof of it every day. Little things, you know? Like the delivery trucks slowing down on the street, or the neighbors looking over at me... it just proves that they know it too. I'm not wrong about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of time where you felt extremely low or depressed, almost the opposite of how you've been feeling with this project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt like that. I mean... I don't get down or depressed. I've just been completely focused on the work. There's way too much to get done with the project to sit around feeling low, so no, I haven't had anything like what you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping less than usual since you started working on this project?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any changes with my sleep. I sleep just fine. When I'm tired, I lay down and go to bed, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm sleeping any less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that most people don't have?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't call it a magic power or anything like that. But... I do have a gift. I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important, something people will travel just to come see. It's because... well, nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just see how it all fits together, plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had these kinds of thoughts about your abilities and importance, or are they relatively new?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about always. I mean... I've always been good at what I do, but this... this is different. It just came to me recently, clear as a bell, what I'm supposed to do with that house. I didn't have this exact idea before, no. It's just... it's happening now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are a famous or important person that most people would know about?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not famous. I mean, people don't know my name right now. I'm just a regular guy who sees what needs to be done with that house. Once I finish it, well... maybe they'll know me then. But right now? No, I'm not anybody famous.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts like these about being specially gifted or having a crucial mission at other times in your life, or are they mostly just about this project?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. It wasn't until I saw what had to be done with the house that I felt like I had this real purpose. So, yeah, most of my life I didn't feel special at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt as energized and confident as you do now, but then later felt extremely low or depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I've had times where I felt down, sure, like anybody else... but never after feeling like this. So, in that way, yes, my mission is something special that only I can do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be wrong about your special mission or the importance of your project?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think it's possible I'm wrong. I know I'm repeating myself here, and I know people probably just think I'm mixed up. But I am absolutely sure this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. \n\nI see the proof of it every day. Little things, you know? Like the delivery trucks slowing down on the street, or the neighbors looking over at me... it just proves that they know it too. I'm not wrong about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of time where you felt extremely low or depressed, almost the opposite of how you've been feeling with this project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt like that. I mean... I don't get down or depressed. I've just been completely focused on the work. There's way too much to get done with the project to sit around feeling low, so no, I haven't had anything like what you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping less than usual since you started working on this project?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any changes with my sleep. I sleep just fine. When I'm tired, I lay down and go to bed, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm sleeping any less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that most people don't have?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't call it a magic power or anything like that. But... I do have a gift. I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important, something people will travel just to come see. It's because... well, nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just see how it all fits together, plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had these kinds of thoughts about your abilities and importance, or are they relatively new?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about always. I mean... I've always been good at what I do, but this... this is different. It just came to me recently, clear as a bell, what I'm supposed to do with that house. I didn't have this exact idea before, no. It's just... it's happening now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are a famous or important person that most people would know about?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not famous. I mean, people don't know my name right now. I'm just a regular guy who sees what needs to be done with that house. Once I finish it, well... maybe they'll know me then. But right now? No, I'm not anybody famous.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts like these about being specially gifted or having a crucial mission at other times in your life, or are they mostly just about this project?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. It's really just this project. I mean... I've worked hard my whole life, just doing my day-to-day, but I never felt like I had a special mission or anything like that before. It's just... when I really looked at that Schiller Park house, it all just made sense to me. So, yeah, it's pretty much just about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you didn't feel as special or gifted as you do now?\n\nPatient: Well, sure. Like I was just saying... pretty much my whole life up until now. I never walked around thinking I was gifted or anything like that. I just... I just did my work, you know? Paid my way. It wasn't until I saw what had to be done with the house that I felt like I had this real purpose. So, yeah, most of my life I didn't feel special at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt as energized and confident as you do now, but then later felt extremely low or depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I've had times where I felt down, sure, like anybody else... but never after feeling like this. I've never felt this energized before in my life, so I've never had that kind of drop-off you're talking about. Like I said, I've just been a regular guy, going to work, coming home. Nothing extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy, energetic, and confident, like you do now, and then later felt extremely low or depressed?\n\nPatient: No, like I just said, I haven't. I've never felt this good before, so I've never had a crash after it. I've had normal days and some sad days, but nothing like what you're asking about."
